\hfill{\small November 2026}

Respected Vice-Chancellors, Deans and Heads of Department,

Between now and 2035, every graduate in engineering, science and management will work beside AI systems, and many will build them. Employers no longer ask whether a student has studied AI; they ask whether the student has \textbf{built, deployed and governed an AI product}. Universities that close this gap first will lead in placements, research output, accreditation outcomes and rankings.

This syllabus was written to close that gap. It offers \textbf{sixteen industry-aligned courses}: ten in core AI, five in emerging technologies and a capstone practice course in \textbf{Forward Deployed Engineering}, the fastest-growing AI role of 2026. Every course has five units, a hands-on outcome for every unit, current industry tools, an enterprise capstone, recommended industry certifications, the skills, jobs and salary outlook it leads to, and the programming languages that will matter through 2035. Ten real-time enterprise products and ten unicorn-track products show how the courses combine into systems that industry runs. It fits NEP 2020's emphasis on multidisciplinary, skill-based and outcome-based learning, and its CO--Bloom mapping supports OBE documentation for NBA and NAAC.

A strong syllabus is only the first step. Results come from trained faculty, working laboratories, real projects and steady review. \textbf{AlgoProfessor AI R\&D Solutions} offers end-to-end consultancy so your institution can adopt this curriculum quickly and deliver it with industry quality:

For Deans & A board-of-studies-ready curriculum across departments, OBE and accreditation evidence, and a measurable plan for faculty capability.\\
For Heads of Department & Course files, lab manuals, GitHub repositories, trained faculty, live student projects and certification-ready students from the first semester of adoption.\\

I invite you to partner with us to make your institution a model of AI-ready higher education for India. Together we can prepare graduates who do not just use AI, but lead it.

With regards,\\[2pt]

\section*{Programme Overview}\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Programme Overview}
This curriculum prepares learners to \textbf{design, build, deploy and govern AI products at enterprise scale}. Every course names its top three programming languages with a 2026--2035 outlook. Every unit ends with a concrete \textbf{hands-on outcome} (a working artefact the learner builds), and every course follows the same five-unit arc: \emph{foundations $\rightarrow$ core methods $\rightarrow$ advanced methods $\rightarrow$ engineering for production $\rightarrow$ enterprise deployment, governance and capstone}. Ten core AI courses are complemented by a five-course \textbf{Emerging Technologies Track} and an \textbf{Industry Practice Track} in Forward Deployed Engineering. Each course pairs 45 lecture hours with 30 hours of tool-driven laboratory work and ends with a capstone that produces a deployable product increment.
{\small\begin{longtable}{@{}p{0.7cm}p{5.0cm}p{1.5cm}p{1.4cm}p{6.3cm}@{}}\hdr\hw{No.}&\hw{Course}&\hw{Code}&\hw{L--T--P--C}&\hw{Product Focus}\\\endhead
1 & \textbf{Artificial Intelligence} & EAI-101 & 3--0--2--4 & From search and reasoning to enterprise decision intelligence services\\
2 & \textbf{Machine Learning} & EAI-102 & 3--0--2--4 & Building, validating and operating predictive models in production\\
3 & \textbf{Deep Learning} & EAI-103 & 3--0--2--4 & Neural architectures engineered for accuracy, scale and eﬀicient inference\\
4 & \textbf{Reinforcement Learning} & EAI-104 & 3--0--2--4 & Sequential decision-making for optimisation, control and AI alignment\\
5 & \textbf{Computer Vision} & EAI-105 & 3--0--2--4 & Visual perception products for inspection, documents, retail and video analytics\\
6 & \textbf{Large Language Models} & EAI-106 & 3--0--2--4 & Pre-training, adaptation, retrieval grounding and LLMOps for enterprise language services\\
7 & \textbf{Agentic AI} & EAI-107 & 3--0--2--4 & Autonomous, tool-using and multi-agent systems that execute enterprise workflows\\
8 & \textbf{Generative AI} & EAI-108 & 3--0--2--4 & Multimodal generation of text, images, audio, video, code and synthetic data\\
9 & \textbf{Data Engineering for AI} & EAI-109 & 3--0--2--4 & Reliable batch, streaming and unstructured-data platforms that feed AI products\\
10 & \textbf{Trustworthy AI} & EAI-110 & 3--0--2--4 & Fair, explainable, robust, private, secure and regulation-ready AI products\\
\multicolumn{5}{@{}c@{}}{\cellcolor{igreen}\color{white}\bfseries Emerging Technologies Track}\\
11 & \textbf{Quantum Computing \& Quantum AI} & EAI-111 & 3--0--2--4 & Quantum algorithms, quantum machine learning and quantum-safe enterprise systems\\
12 & \textbf{Edge AI, TinyML \& AIoT} & EAI-112 & 3--0--2--4 & On-device intelligence for sensors, wearables, vehicles, factories and smart infrastructure\\
13 & \textbf{Physical AI \& Intelligent Robotics} & EAI-113 & 3--0--2--4 & Embodied AI that perceives, plans and acts in the physical world\\
14 & \textbf{AI-Driven Cybersecurity} & EAI-114 & 3--0--2--4 & Machine learning and agents for threat detection, response and secure enterprises\\
15 & \textbf{Blockchain \& Decentralised AI} & EAI-115 & 3--0--2--4 & Verifiable data, model provenance and decentralised trust for AI ecosystems\\
\multicolumn{5}{@{}c@{}}{\cellcolor{gold}\color{white}\bfseries Industry Practice Track}\\
16 & \textbf{Forward Deployed Engineering (FDE)} & EAI-116 & 3--0--2--4 & Embedding with enterprise clients to discover, build, deploy and scale AI solutions that deliver measurable business value\\
\bottomrule\end{longtable}}

\subsection*{Common Product-Engineering Standards}
\begin{itemize}\small
\item Git-based version control, code review and CI/CD on every lab
\item Containerised delivery with Docker; Kubernetes for capstones
\item Experiment tracking, model registry and reproducible environments
\item Model cards, data sheets and risk register for each product
\item Cloud parity: AWS, Azure or GCP; on-prem GPU where available
\item Each course maps to current industry certifications (checked November 2026)
\end{itemize}
\end{multicols}
\subsection*{Industry Certification Policy}
{\small Every course lists industry certifications that match its units, from AWS, Microsoft, Google Cloud, NVIDIA, Databricks, IBM, CompTIA, ISACA, IAPP, the Linux Foundation and others, plus NPTEL/SWAYAM where it fits. Names and exam codes were checked in November 2026. Several well-known exams were retired in 2026 (AWS Machine Learning -- Specialty; Microsoft AI-102, DP-100 and AI-900), so their current replacements are listed instead. Vendors change exams often, so learners should confirm the exam code on the issuer's website before registering. Course certificates (Coursera, edX, NVIDIA DLI) are listed as useful extras, not as replacements for proctored exams.}
\subsection*{Learning Pathway}
{\small{\color{gold}\bfseries Foundation track:} AI $\rightarrow$ ML $\rightarrow$ Data Engineering \quad {\color{gold}\bfseries Model track:} DL $\rightarrow$ CV / RL \quad {\color{gold}\bfseries Frontier track:} LLM $\rightarrow$ Generative AI $\rightarrow$ Agentic AI \quad {\color{gold}\bfseries Cross-cutting:} Trustworthy AI is taken alongside or after any frontier course.\\
{\color{igreent}\bfseries Emerging track:} DL $\rightarrow$ Edge AI / Physical AI \quad ML $\rightarrow$ Quantum AI \quad ML $\rightarrow$ AI Cybersecurity $\rightarrow$ Blockchain \& Decentralised AI\\
{\color{gold}\bfseries Practice track:} LLM + Agentic AI + Data Engineering $\rightarrow$ Forward Deployed Engineering, taken in the final year as the client-embedded capstone of the programme.}

\section*{Programming Language Strategy 2026--2035}\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Programming Language Strategy 2026--2035}
Every course lists the three programming languages that matter most for building its products, with an outlook to 2035. The plan follows one principle: \emph{Python for intelligence, a systems language for performance and safety, and a delivery language for users and platforms}. Graduates who can work across all three layers can build complete AI products, not just models.
\par{\small\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}>{\bfseries}p{3.0cm}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{4.2cm}Y@{}}\hdr\hw{Language}&\hw{Courses (top-3)}&\hw{Why it matters for 2026--2035 engineering}\\
Python & 1--14, 16 & The working language of AI: models, agents, data and evaluation. AI coding assistants make it even more productive.\\
\rowcolor{rowbg}C++ / CUDA & 1, 3, 4, 5, 8, 11, 12, 13 & The performance layer: GPU kernels, inference engines, simulators, robots and edge devices. Demand rises with compute cost.\\
Rust & 6, 10, 12, 13, 14, 15 & Memory-safe systems language for AI infrastructure, security, embedded and blockchain; fastest-rising systems language.\\
\rowcolor{rowbg}SQL & 2, 9, 16 & The most durable data skill; every AI product depends on well-modelled data.\\
TypeScript / JavaScript & 6, 7, 8, 16 & Delivers AI to users: web apps, chat interfaces, MCP servers and agent SDKs.\\
\rowcolor{rowbg}Go & 7, 14, 15 & Cloud-native services, agent platforms, security tools and enterprise blockchain.\\
Java / Scala & 1, 9 & JVM backbone of enterprise systems and big-data streaming engines.\\
\rowcolor{rowbg}Domain languages & R (2, 10), Julia (4), OpenQASM 3 (11), Solidity (15), Mojo (3, emerging) & Specialist languages for statistics, scientific computing, quantum circuits, smart contracts and next-generation AI performance.\\
\bottomrule\end{tabularx}}
\subsection*{Engineering Outcomes Expected by 2030--2035}
\begin{itemize}\small
\item \textbf{AI-assisted software engineering is the norm:} engineers direct coding agents, so code review, testing, specification and system design matter more than typing code.
\item \textbf{Polyglot by default:} a Python model is served by C++/Rust engines, reached through TypeScript or Go services, and fed by SQL pipelines; graduates must read and reason across these layers.
\item \textbf{Memory safety and security by design:} security guidance increasingly favours memory-safe languages such as Rust for new critical systems.
\item \textbf{Performance engineering is a core skill:} GPU, edge and quantum hardware reward engineers who can optimise below the Python layer.
\item \textbf{Specification and verification:} formal contracts, typed interfaces and evaluation suites become the way humans control AI-written code.
\end{itemize}
{\footnotesize\itshape\color{muted} The 2026--2035 outlook reflects current industry direction and is a planning guide, not a forecast guarantee; review it every academic year.}

\subsection*{Core Competencies}\begin{itemize}\small
\item Formulate business problems as search, constraint-satisfaction, planning or probabilistic-inference tasks.
\item Build knowledge representations (ontologies, knowledge graphs, rules) that power explainable decisions.
\item Combine symbolic reasoning with learned models in neuro-symbolic and LLM-assisted architectures.
\item Package AI reasoning components as scalable, testable, versioned microservices.
\end{itemize}
\subsection*{Five-Unit Curriculum}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT I}\quad Foundations of Intelligent Systems\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small AI history and the modern AI stack; intelligent agents and PEAS; environment types; rational agent design; AI product life cycle (discovery, feasibility, MVP, scale); enterprise AI use-case canvas; problem framing, success metrics and ROI estimation; build-vs-buy-vs-API decisions.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Builds a simulated warehouse agent and an AI use-case canvas with KPIs and ROI estimate for a real business problem.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT II}\quad Search, Optimisation and Constraint Solving\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Uninformed search (BFS, DFS, uniform-cost, iterative deepening); informed search (greedy, A*, admissible heuristics); local search (hill climbing, simulated annealing, genetic algorithms); adversarial search (minimax, alpha–beta, MCTS); constraint-satisfaction problems; integer and linear programming for scheduling, routing and resource allocation.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Delivers an A* route planner and an OR-Tools shift scheduler that beat a manual baseline on cost and time.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT III}\quad Knowledge Representation and Reasoning\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Propositional and first-order logic; inference, resolution and forward/backward chaining; rule engines; ontologies (RDF, OWL) and enterprise knowledge graphs; SPARQL and Cypher querying; semantic search over knowledge graphs; neuro-symbolic reasoning and LLM + knowledge-graph integration (GraphRAG).\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Creates a Neo4j product knowledge graph with Cypher queries and a rule engine that explains every inference.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT IV}\quad Uncertainty, Planning and Decision Making\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Probability review; Bayesian networks and exact/approximate inference; hidden Markov models; decision theory and utility; Markov decision processes; classical planning (STRIPS, PDDL); hierarchical task networks; multi-criteria decision analysis for business decision support.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Builds a Bayesian loan-risk network and a PDDL planner that generates an automated IToperations runbook.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT V}\quad AI Systems Engineering and Enterprise Deployment\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Architecture of decision-intelligence platforms; AI services as REST/gRPC APIs; latency, caching and scaling; testing AI components (unit, property-based, scenario tests); human-in-the-loop design; monitoring decision quality; AI risk classification and documentation; case studies in supply chain, banking and healthcare.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Ships a containerised decision API (FastAPI + Docker) with CI tests, latency benchmarks and a decision log.\end{tcolorbox}

\phantomsection\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Course 2\quad Machine Learning}
\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=navy,colframe=navy,arc=2pt,left=8pt,top=5pt,bottom=5pt]{\small\bfseries\color{goldl}COURSE 2 \;|\; EAI-102}\\[1pt]{\LARGE\bfseries\color{white}Machine Learning}\\[2pt]{\small\itshape\color{goldl}Building, validating and operating predictive models in production}\end{tcolorbox}
\par{\small\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}Y>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}p{2.6cm}@{}}Course Code & EAI-102 & L--T--P--C & 3--0--2--4\\Prerequisites & Python, linear algebra, probability \& statistics & Contact Hours & 45 L + 30 P\\\end{tabularx}}
\subsection*{Core Competencies}\begin{itemize}\small
\item Translate business KPIs into ML objectives, labels and evaluation metrics.
\item Engineer features and build robust supervised, unsupervised and ensemble models on tabular data.
\item Validate models rigorously: leakage control, cross-validation, calibration, cost-sensitive metrics.
\item Operate models with MLOps: tracking, registry, CI/CD, serving, drift monitoring and retraining.
\end{itemize}
\subsection*{Five-Unit Curriculum}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT I}\quad ML Foundations and Problem Framing\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Learning paradigms; the ML product life cycle; data collection, labelling and sampling; bias–variance trade-off; loss functions and empirical risk minimisation; gradient descent variants; train/validation/test design; baseline models; mapping business KPIs to ML metrics.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Produces an EDA and data-quality report and a baseline model with a documented KPI-to-metric mapping.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT II}\quad Supervised Learning\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Linear and logistic regression with regularisation (L1, L2, elastic net); k-NN; naive Bayes; support vector machines and kernels; decision trees; ensembles: bagging, random forests, gradient boosting (XGBoost, LightGBM, CatBoost); handling class imbalance; probability calibration.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Trains a calibrated credit-scoring model and a gradient-boosted churn model that outperform baselines.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT III}\quad Unsupervised Learning and Representation\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Clustering (k-means, hierarchical, DBSCAN, Gaussian mixtures); dimensionality reduction (PCA, t-SNE, UMAP); anomaly detection (isolation forest, one-class SVM); association rules; recommender systems (collaborative filtering, matrix factorisation); time-series forecasting fundamentals.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Delivers customer segments with UMAP visualisation, an anomaly detector and a working recommender.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT IV}\quad Model Evaluation, Tuning and Interpretability\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Metrics: precision/recall, F1, ROC-AUC, PR-AUC, RMSE, MAPE, lift and business cost curves; k-fold, stratified and time-series cross-validation; data leakage; hyper-parameter optimisation (grid, random, Bayesian, Optuna); feature engineering and selection; SHAP and permutation importance; A/B and offline–online evaluation.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Produces an Optuna-tuned model with leakage checks, cost-curve analysis and a SHAP explanation report.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT V}\quad MLOps and Enterprise Deployment\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Reproducible pipelines; experiment tracking and model registry; feature stores; batch vs real-time serving; model packaging (ONNX, BentoML); CI/CD/CT for ML; data and concept drift detection; model monitoring SLAs; retraining strategies; ML governance and model documentation.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Deploys an MLflow-tracked model via BentoML with Evidently drift alerts and an automated retraining trigger.\end{tcolorbox}

\phantomsection\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Course 3\quad Deep Learning}
\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=navy,colframe=navy,arc=2pt,left=8pt,top=5pt,bottom=5pt]{\small\bfseries\color{goldl}COURSE 3 \;|\; EAI-103}\\[1pt]{\LARGE\bfseries\color{white}Deep Learning}\\[2pt]{\small\itshape\color{goldl}Neural architectures engineered for accuracy, scale and eﬀicient inference}\end{tcolorbox}
\par{\small\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}Y>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}p{2.6cm}@{}}Course Code & EAI-103 & L--T--P--C & 3--0--2--4\\Prerequisites & EAI-102 Machine Learning, calculus, linear algebra & Contact Hours & 45 L + 30 P\\\end{tabularx}}
\subsection*{Core Competencies}\begin{itemize}\small
\item Design, train and debug neural networks with modern optimisation and regularisation practice.
\item Select architectures (CNN, RNN, Transformer, GNN) suited to image, sequence, text and graph data.
\item Scale training with mixed precision and distributed data/model parallelism on GPU clusters.
\item Compress and optimise models for low-latency inference on cloud and edge targets.
\end{itemize}
\subsection*{Five-Unit Curriculum}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT I}\quad Neural Network Foundations\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Perceptron and multilayer perceptrons; activation functions; universal approximation; computational graphs and automatic differentiation; backpropagation; loss functions; weight initialisation; tensors and GPU computing with PyTorch; the deep-learning project workflow.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Implements an MLP from scratch with autograd on GPU and verifies gradients against PyTorch.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT II}\quad Optimisation and Regularisation\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small SGD, momentum, Adam, AdamW; learning-rate schedules and warm-up; batch and layer normalisation; dropout, weight decay, data augmentation, early stopping; vanishing/exploding gradients; residual connections; debugging training: loss curves, gradient checks, overfitting a single batch.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Produces an optimiser/LR-schedule comparison study and a debugged, stable training recipe.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT III}\quad Convolutional and Sequence Architectures\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Convolution, pooling and receptive fields; LeNet, VGG, ResNet, EﬀicientNet; transfer learning and fine-tuning; recurrent networks, LSTM and GRU; sequence-to-sequence models; temporal convolution networks; time-series and sensor applications.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Fine-tunes a ResNet product classifier and an LSTM sensor forecaster with reported accuracy and error.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT IV}\quad Attention, Transformers and Graph Networks\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Attention mechanism; self-attention and multi-head attention; the Transformer encoder–decoder; positional encodings; BERT- and GPT-style pre-training; Vision Transformers; graph neural networks (GCN, GraphSAGE, GAT); self-supervised and contrastive learning.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Fine-tunes a Transformer text classifier and trains a GNN fraud detector on a transaction graph.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT V}\quad Scalable Training and Production Inference\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Mixed-precision training; distributed data parallel and FSDP; experiment management; model compression: quantisation, pruning, knowledge distillation; export to ONNX and TensorRT; inference servers; edge deployment; cost, latency and throughput benchmarking; model cards.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Trains with DDP + mixed precision, quantises to INT8 and serves on Triton with an accuracy-latency-cost report.\end{tcolorbox}

\phantomsection\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Course 4\quad Reinforcement Learning}
\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=navy,colframe=navy,arc=2pt,left=8pt,top=5pt,bottom=5pt]{\small\bfseries\color{goldl}COURSE 4 \;|\; EAI-104}\\[1pt]{\LARGE\bfseries\color{white}Reinforcement Learning}\\[2pt]{\small\itshape\color{goldl}Sequential decision-making for optimisation, control and AI alignment}\end{tcolorbox}
\par{\small\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}Y>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}p{2.6cm}@{}}Course Code & EAI-104 & L--T--P--C & 3--0--2--4\\Prerequisites & EAI-102, EAI-103, probability & Contact Hours & 45 L + 30 P\\\end{tabularx}}
\subsection*{Core Competencies}\begin{itemize}\small
\item Model business processes as Markov decision processes with well-shaped rewards.
\item Implement tabular, value-based, policy-gradient and actor–critic algorithms.
\item Apply offline RL, bandits and simulation to settings where live exploration is costly.
\item Use RL from human feedback and preference optimisation to align language models.
\end{itemize}
\subsection*{Five-Unit Curriculum}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT I}\quad RL Foundations and MDPs\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small The agent–environment interface; rewards, returns and discounting; Markov decision processes; policies and value functions; Bellman equations; dynamic programming: policy evaluation, policy iteration, value iteration; exploration vs exploitation; multi-armed and contextual bandits for recommendation and pricing.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Solves an inventory MDP with value iteration and deploys a contextual bandit for offer recommendation.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT II}\quad Model-Free Tabular Methods\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Monte Carlo prediction and control; temporal-difference learning; SARSA and Q-learning; n-step and eligibility traces; on-policy vs off-policy learning; importance sampling; reward design and shaping; building simulation environments with Gymnasium.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Builds a custom Gymnasium environment and a Q-learning agent with a shaped reward function.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT III}\quad Deep Reinforcement Learning\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Function approximation; Deep Q-Networks, experience replay and target networks; Double and Dueling DQN; policy-gradient theorem and REINFORCE; actor–critic, A2C/A3C; PPO and TRPO; DDPG, TD3 and SAC for continuous control.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Trains DQN and PPO agents and compares sample eﬀiciency and stability on standard benchmarks.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT IV}\quad Advanced and Offline RL\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Model-based RL and planning; offline/batch RL (CQL, IQL); safe and constrained RL; multi-agent RL; hierarchical RL; imitation learning and inverse RL; sim-to-real transfer; evaluation of RL policies (off-policy evaluation, confidence bounds).\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Trains an offline RL policy on logged data and evaluates it with off-policy estimators before deployment.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT V}\quad RL for Enterprise and LLM Alignment\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small RL in dynamic pricing, inventory, ad bidding, energy management and robotics; reward modelling; RL from human feedback (RLHF); direct preference optimisation (DPO) and GRPO-style methods; RL for reasoning and agentic models; deploying policies with guardrails, shadow mode and roll-back.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Aligns a small LLM with a reward model + DPO and runs a pricing policy in shadow mode with guardrails.\end{tcolorbox}

\phantomsection\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Course 5\quad Computer Vision}
\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=navy,colframe=navy,arc=2pt,left=8pt,top=5pt,bottom=5pt]{\small\bfseries\color{goldl}COURSE 5 \;|\; EAI-105}\\[1pt]{\LARGE\bfseries\color{white}Computer Vision}\\[2pt]{\small\itshape\color{goldl}Visual perception products for inspection, documents, retail and video analytics}\end{tcolorbox}
\par{\small\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}Y>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}p{2.6cm}@{}}Course Code & EAI-105 & L--T--P--C & 3--0--2--4\\Prerequisites & EAI-103 Deep Learning, linear algebra & Contact Hours & 45 L + 30 P\\\end{tabularx}}
\subsection*{Core Competencies}\begin{itemize}\small
\item Apply classical image processing and geometry for robust pre-processing and measurement.
\item Build detection, segmentation, tracking and OCR pipelines with modern deep models.
\item Use vision foundation models and vision–language models for zero-shot and few-shot tasks.
\item Deploy real-time vision systems on edge devices and video-streaming infrastructure.
\end{itemize}
\subsection*{Five-Unit Curriculum}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT I}\quad Image Formation and Classical Vision\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Image formation, colour spaces and camera models; filtering, edges and morphology; histogram processing; feature detectors and descriptors (Harris, SIFT, ORB); image alignment and homography; camera calibration; image-quality and data-acquisition planning for industrial settings.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Calibrates a camera and builds a measurement/stitching tool using classical features and homography.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT II}\quad Deep Image Classification and Representation\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small CNN backbones for vision; transfer learning; data augmentation and synthetic data; Vision Transformers; selfsupervised visual representation (contrastive, masked image modelling); metric learning and visual search; label quality and active learning.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Delivers a defect classifier via transfer learning and a visual-search service using embeddings.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT III}\quad Detection, Segmentation and Tracking\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Object detection: two-stage (Faster R-CNN) and one-stage (YOLO family, DETR); semantic, instance and panoptic segmentation (U-Net, Mask R-CNN); promptable segmentation (SAM); keypoints and pose estimation; multi-object tracking; evaluation metrics (IoU, mAP, MOTA).\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Trains a YOLO detector and SAM-assisted segmentation on custom data and a people-counting tracker (mAP, MOTA reported).\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT IV}\quad 3D, Video and Vision–Language Models\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Stereo, depth estimation and point clouds; 3D reconstruction basics; video understanding and action recognition; OCR and document AI (layout analysis, key–value extraction); CLIP and open-vocabulary detection; vision–language models for visual question answering and captioning.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Builds an invoice OCR + key-field extraction pipeline and a zero-shot CLIP/VLM classification demo.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT V}\quad Edge Deployment and Vision Product Engineering\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Real-time video pipelines (RTSP , GStreamer, DeepStream); model optimisation for edge (TensorRT, OpenVINO, TFLite); edge hardware (Jetson, NPUs); MLOps for vision: data versioning, labelling workflows, drift; privacy-preserving vision (face blurring, on-device processing); case studies in manufacturing, retail and healthcare.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Deploys a real-time RTSP vision pipeline on Jetson with TensorRT, face blurring and an events dashboard.\end{tcolorbox}

\phantomsection\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Course 6\quad Large Language Models}
\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=navy,colframe=navy,arc=2pt,left=8pt,top=5pt,bottom=5pt]{\small\bfseries\color{goldl}COURSE 6 \;|\; EAI-106}\\[1pt]{\LARGE\bfseries\color{white}Large Language Models}\\[2pt]{\small\itshape\color{goldl}Pre-training, adaptation, retrieval grounding and LLMOps for enterprise language services}\end{tcolorbox}
\par{\small\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}Y>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}p{2.6cm}@{}}Course Code & EAI-106 & L--T--P--C & 3--0--2--4\\Prerequisites & EAI-103 Deep Learning, NLP basics & Contact Hours & 45 L + 30 P\\\end{tabularx}}
\subsection*{Core Competencies}\begin{itemize}\small
\item Explain how Transformer language models are tokenised, pre-trained, scaled and aligned.
\item Adapt open and proprietary models through prompting, retrieval and parameter-eﬀicient fine-tuning.
\item Build retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems grounded in enterprise knowledge with citations.
\item Serve, evaluate, secure and cost-optimise LLM applications in production (LLMOps).
\end{itemize}
\subsection*{Five-Unit Curriculum}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT I}\quad Language Modelling Foundations\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small From n-grams to neural language models; tokenisation (BPE, WordPiece, SentencePiece); embeddings; the decoder-only Transformer; causal attention and KV cache; pre-training objectives and data pipelines; scaling laws; mixture-of-experts; the open vs closed model landscape and licensing.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Trains a BPE tokeniser and a mini-GPT from scratch and measures perplexity and KV-cache speed-up.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT II}\quad Prompting, Reasoning and Structured Output\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Prompt design patterns; zero-/few-shot and in-context learning; chain-of-thought and reasoning models; system prompts and role design; structured outputs (JSON schema, function calling); prompt versioning and prompt testing; context-window management and long-context strategies.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Builds a versioned prompt library with JSON-schema outputs and an automated prompt regression test suite.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT III}\quad Fine-Tuning and Alignment\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Supervised fine-tuning and instruction tuning; parameter-eﬀicient fine-tuning (LoRA, QLoRA, adapters); dataset curation and synthetic instruction data; preference alignment (RLHF, DPO); distillation into small language models; catastrophic forgetting; domain adaptation for legal, finance and healthcare text.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Fine-tunes a 7-8B model with QLoRA and aligns it with DPO, with before/after evaluation.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT IV}\quad Retrieval-Augmented Generation\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Embedding models and semantic search; chunking strategies; vector databases and indexes (HNSW, IVF); hybrid search (BM25 + dense) and re-ranking; query rewriting; GraphRAG and multi-hop retrieval; grounding and citation; access-control-aware retrieval over enterprise documents.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Delivers a hybrid-search RAG assistant with re-ranking, citations and permission-aware retrieval.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT V}\quad LLMOps, Evaluation and Production Serving\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Inference optimisation: quantisation (GPTQ, A WQ), continuous batching, paged attention, speculative decoding; serving engines; LLM gateways, routing and caching; evaluation (task metrics, LLM-as-judge, RAG faithfulness); observability and tracing; token-cost management; prompt-injection and data-leakage defences.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Serves a quantised model on vLLM behind a gateway with RAGAS evaluation, tracing and token-cost dashboards.\end{tcolorbox}

\item How transformers and tokenisers work inside an LLM
\item Prompt engineering, reasoning patterns and structured (JSON) outputs
\item Fine-tuning with LoRA/QLoRA on domain data
\item Retrieval-augmented generation with embeddings and vector databases
\item LLM evaluation: accuracy, faithfulness, safety and cost
\item Guardrails against hallucination and prompt injection
\item Serving LLMs efficiently (vLLM, quantisation, caching)
\item LLMOps: tracing, versioning and production monitoring
\end{itemize}\end{multicols}

\phantomsection\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Course 7\quad Agentic AI}
\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=navy,colframe=navy,arc=2pt,left=8pt,top=5pt,bottom=5pt]{\small\bfseries\color{goldl}COURSE 7 \;|\; EAI-107}\\[1pt]{\LARGE\bfseries\color{white}Agentic AI}\\[2pt]{\small\itshape\color{goldl}Autonomous, tool-using and multi-agent systems that execute enterprise workflows}\end{tcolorbox}
\par{\small\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}Y>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}p{2.6cm}@{}}Course Code & EAI-107 & L--T--P--C & 3--0--2--4\\Prerequisites & EAI-106 Large Language Models, software engineering & Contact Hours & 45 L + 30 P\\\end{tabularx}}
\subsection*{Core Competencies}\begin{itemize}\small
\item Design agent architectures that plan, use tools, keep memory and recover from errors.
\item Choose correctly between deterministic workflows, single agents and multi-agent systems.
\item Integrate agents with enterprise systems through standard tool protocols and secure APIs.
\item Evaluate, observe, sandbox and govern agents with human approval for consequential actions.
\end{itemize}
\subsection*{Five-Unit Curriculum}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT I}\quad Foundations of AI Agents\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small From classical agents to LLM agents; the perceive–reason–act loop; autonomy levels; workflows vs agents; agentic design patterns: prompt chaining, routing, parallelisation, orchestrator–workers, evaluator–optimiser; ReAct and reflection; when not to build an agent; cost, latency and reliability trade-offs.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Builds a ReAct agent with tools and a design note justifying workflow vs agent for a business task.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT II}\quad Tools, Planning and Memory\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Function/tool calling and tool schema design; the Model Context Protocol (MCP) for connecting tools and data; code-execution and browser/computer-use tools; planning and task decomposition; self-correction and retries; short-term, long-term and episodic memory; state management and checkpoints.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Builds and consumes an MCP server for a CRM and an agent with long-term memory and checkpoints.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT III}\quad Multi-Agent Systems and Orchestration\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Multi-agent architectures: supervisor, hierarchical, peer-to-peer, swarm; role and hand-off design; agent-to-agent communication protocols; graph-based orchestration and durable execution; conflict resolution and consensus; human-in-the-loop interrupts and approvals; long-running background agents.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Delivers a multi-agent research team with supervisor hand-offs and human-approval interrupts.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT IV}\quad Agent Evaluation, Observability and Reliability\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Task-success metrics and benchmark design; trajectory evaluation; tool-call accuracy; regression test suites for agents; tracing and telemetry (OpenTelemetry); failure-mode analysis (loops, hallucinated tools, goal drift); guardrails, budgets and timeouts; red-teaming agents for prompt injection and excessive agency.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Produces an agent evaluation suite with trajectory traces, failure-mode analysis and a red-team report.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT V}\quad Enterprise Agent Platforms and Deployment\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Agent platform architecture: identity, permissions and least privilege; sandboxed execution; secrets handling; audit logs; integration with ERP, CRM, ITSM and data platforms; agent cost governance; deployment patterns (serverless, containers, queues); case studies in customer support, IT operations, finance and software engineering.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Deploys a sandboxed, least-privilege enterprise agent with audit logs, budgets and an integration to ITSM/CRM.\end{tcolorbox}

\phantomsection\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Course 8\quad Generative AI}
\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=navy,colframe=navy,arc=2pt,left=8pt,top=5pt,bottom=5pt]{\small\bfseries\color{goldl}COURSE 8 \;|\; EAI-108}\\[1pt]{\LARGE\bfseries\color{white}Generative AI}\\[2pt]{\small\itshape\color{goldl}Multimodal generation of text, images, audio, video, code and synthetic data}\end{tcolorbox}
\par{\small\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}Y>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}p{2.6cm}@{}}Course Code & EAI-108 & L--T--P--C & 3--0--2--4\\Prerequisites & EAI-103 Deep Learning, probability & Contact Hours & 45 L + 30 P\\\end{tabularx}}
\subsection*{Core Competencies}\begin{itemize}\small
\item Explain the probabilistic foundations of V AEs, GANs, autoregressive, flow and diffusion models.
\item Build and customise image, audio, video and multimodal generation pipelines.
\item Generate privacy-preserving synthetic data and evaluate its fidelity and utility.
\item Ship generative products with content provenance, safety filters and IP-aware governance.
\end{itemize}
\subsection*{Five-Unit Curriculum}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT I}\quad Foundations of Generative Modelling\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Discriminative vs generative models; likelihood, latent variables and the ELBO; autoencoders and variational autoencoders; autoregressive models; normalising flows; evaluation of generative models (FID, IS, CLIP score, human evaluation); the generative AI product landscape.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Trains a V AE on product images and explores its latent space with FID-based evaluation.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT II}\quad Adversarial and Diffusion Models\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small GANs: minimax objective, DCGAN, conditional GAN, StyleGAN, training instabilities; denoising diffusion probabilistic models; score-based models; samplers (DDIM, DPM-Solver); classifier-free guidance; latent diffusion; diffusion Transformers and flow matching.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Implements DDPM from scratch and a conditional GAN that generates synthetic defect images.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT III}\quad Controllable Image and Multimodal Generation\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Text-to-image systems; ControlNet and conditioning; image editing and inpainting; personalisation (DreamBooth, LoRA, textual inversion); multimodal models that understand and generate across text and images; text-to-video and image-to-video generation; brand-consistent creative pipelines.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Builds a ControlNet text-to-image pipeline and a LoRA-personalised brand-asset generator.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT IV}\quad Audio, Code and Synthetic Data Generation\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Speech synthesis and voice models; music and audio generation; speech-to-text; code generation models and AI coding assistants; synthetic tabular data (CTGAN, copulas) and synthetic text data; differential privacy for synthetic data; fidelity, utility and privacy evaluation of synthetic datasets.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Delivers a speech-to-text/text-to-speech pipeline and a privacy-evaluated synthetic tabular dataset.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT V}\quad GenAI Product Engineering and Governance\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Generative application architecture; serving diffusion models at scale; latency/cost optimisation (distillation, fewstep sampling); content safety classifiers; watermarking and content provenance (C2PA); copyright and licensing of training data and outputs; deepfake risks and disclosure; GenAI use-cases in marketing, design, media, e-commerce and education.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Ships a GenAI web app with safety filtering, watermarking and C2PA provenance on every output.\end{tcolorbox}

Provenance \& safety & C2PA tools, invisible watermarking libraries, safety classifiers\\
\end{tabularx}}

\item Multimodal generation across text, image, audio and video
\item Code generation and synthetic data for training and testing
\item Evaluating generative quality, diversity and bias
\item Watermarking, provenance and content safety
\item Building GenAI product features with front-end delivery

\par{\small\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}Y>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}p{2.6cm}@{}}Course Code & EAI-109 & L--T--P--C & 3--0--2--4\\Prerequisites & Python, SQL, database systems & Contact Hours & 45 L + 30 P\\\end{tabularx}}
\subsection*{Core Competencies}\begin{itemize}\small
\item Design data architectures (warehouse, lake, lakehouse, data mesh) for analytics and AI workloads.
\item Build reliable batch and streaming pipelines with orchestration, testing and data contracts.
\item Prepare AI-ready data: feature pipelines, unstructured-data processing and vector indexing.
\item Run data platforms with quality, lineage, security, cost control and regulatory compliance.
\end{itemize}
\subsection*{Five-Unit Curriculum}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT I}\quad Data Engineering Foundations\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small The data engineering life cycle; OLTP vs OLAP; data modelling (normalisation, dimensional modelling, star/snowflake, data vault); advanced SQL and window functions; file formats (CSV , JSON, Parquet, Avro); data warehouses, data lakes and lakehouses; data mesh and data-as-a-product.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Designs a dimensional model and writes advanced SQL analytics over Parquet data in DuckDB.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT II}\quad Batch Processing and Lakehouse Platforms\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small ETL vs ELT; distributed processing with Apache Spark (DataFrames, partitioning, joins, tuning); open table formats (Delta Lake, Apache Iceberg, Apache Hudi); medallion architecture (bronze/silver/gold); transformation with dbt; change data capture; slowly changing dimensions.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Builds a Spark ETL into a Delta/Iceberg medallion lakehouse with tested dbt transformations.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT III}\quad Streaming and Real-Time Data\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Event-driven architecture; Apache Kafka (topics, partitions, consumer groups, exactly-once semantics); schema registry; stream processing with Spark Structured Streaming and Apache Flink; windowing and late data; real-time analytics stores; streaming features for online ML inference.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Streams CDC events from PostgreSQL to Kafka and computes real-time windowed aggregates with Flink.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT IV}\quad Data for AI: Features, Unstructured Data and Vectors\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Feature engineering pipelines and feature stores (offline/online consistency); training-data versioning; document parsing and chunking pipelines for LLMs; embeddings at scale; vector databases and index maintenance; data labelling operations; dataset documentation for AI.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Delivers a document-to-vector ingestion pipeline and a feature store with offline/online consistency.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT V}\quad DataOps, Quality and Governance\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Workflow orchestration; CI/CD for data; data quality testing and data contracts; observability, lineage (OpenLineage) and cataloguing; access control, masking and encryption; privacy regulation (India DPDP Act 2023, GDPR); cost optimisation (FinOps) on cloud data platforms; infrastructure as code.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Orchestrates an Airflow DAG with data-quality gates, OpenLineage lineage, masking and a cost dashboard.\end{tcolorbox}

\phantomsection\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Course 10\quad Trustworthy AI}
\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=navy,colframe=navy,arc=2pt,left=8pt,top=5pt,bottom=5pt]{\small\bfseries\color{goldl}COURSE 10 \;|\; EAI-110}\\[1pt]{\LARGE\bfseries\color{white}Trustworthy AI}\\[2pt]{\small\itshape\color{goldl}Fair, explainable, robust, private, secure and regulation-ready AI products}\end{tcolorbox}
\par{\small\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}Y>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}p{2.6cm}@{}}Course Code & EAI-110 & L--T--P--C & 3--0--2--4\\Prerequisites & EAI-102 Machine Learning; EAI-106 recommended & Contact Hours & 45 L + 30 P\\\end{tabularx}}
\subsection*{Core Competencies}\begin{itemize}\small
\item Identify, measure and mitigate bias and harm across the AI life cycle.
\item Explain model behaviour to developers, auditors and affected users.
\item Secure AI and LLM systems against adversarial, privacy and supply-chain attacks.
\item Operationalise AI governance aligned to the EU AI Act, NIST AI RMF and ISO/IEC 42001.
\end{itemize}
\subsection*{Five-Unit Curriculum}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT I}\quad Principles and Risk Foundations\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Pillars of trustworthy AI: fairness, transparency, accountability, robustness, privacy, safety, sustainability; AI ethics frameworks (OECD, UNESCO, India’s AI governance guidelines); socio-technical harms; stakeholder analysis; AI incident databases and case studies; risk identification across the AI life cycle.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Produces an AI risk register and stakeholder harm analysis for a real AI product.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT II}\quad Fairness and Explainability\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Sources of bias in data and models; group and individual fairness metrics (demographic parity, equalised odds, calibration); impossibility results; pre-, in- and post-processing mitigation; interpretable models; post-hoc explanation (SHAP, LIME, integrated gradients, counterfactuals); explaining LLM outputs; evaluating explanation quality.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Delivers a Fairlearn bias audit with mitigation, a fairness-accuracy trade-off and an explanation report.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT III}\quad Robustness, Privacy and Safety\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Adversarial examples and robust training; distribution shift and out-of-distribution detection; uncertainty quantification and conformal prediction; privacy attacks (membership inference, model inversion); differential privacy; federated learning; LLM safety: hallucination, harmful content, jailbreaks and red-teaming.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Trains a differentially private model, adversarially hardens a classifier and adds conformal uncertainty.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT IV}\quad AI and LLM Security\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Threat modelling for AI systems; data and model poisoning; model extraction; prompt injection (direct and indirect); insecure tool use and excessive agency in agents; AI supply-chain security (model provenance, signed artefacts, SBOM/AIBOM); guardrails and output filtering; security testing with OW ASP and MITRE ATLAS guidance.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Red-teams an LLM application with garak/PyRIT and implements guardrails that close the findings.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT V}\quad Governance, Regulation and Assurance\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small EU AI Act risk tiers and obligations; NIST AI Risk Management Framework; ISO/IEC 42001 AI management systems; India DPDP Act 2023; model cards, data sheets and impact assessments; AI audits and conformity assessment; continuous monitoring and incident response; building a responsible-AI programme and review board.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Produces a model card, impact assessment and EU AI Act / NIST AI RMF readiness checklist.\end{tcolorbox}

Governance & Model Card Toolkit, Credo AI / open governance templates, MLflow model registry\\
\end{tabularx}}

L4 & Adversarial attacks and robust training with ART & L8 & Model card, impact assessment and EU AI Act checklist\\

CO1 & Explain trustworthy-AI principles and identify risks across the AI life cycle. & L2 Understand\\

CO5 & Design a governance and assurance package that meets regulatory requirements. & L6 Create\\

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=goldbg,colframe=goldl,boxrule=0.7pt,arc=2pt,title={Enterprise Capstone Product},colbacktitle=goldl!35,coltitle=navy,fonttitle=\small\bfseries]\small\textbf{Responsible-AI Assurance Toolkit:} an audit pipeline that takes a model or LLM application and produces fairness, explainability, robustness, privacy and red-team results, plus a model card, impact assessment and regulatory-readiness report for an AI review board.\end{tcolorbox}
\subsection*{Skills You Will Gain}\begin{multicols}{2}\begin{itemize}\small
\item AI risk assessment and impact analysis
\item Measuring and mitigating bias and unfairness
\item Explainability methods for models and LLMs
\item Robustness, privacy (differential privacy) and safety testing
\item Security for AI: prompt injection, data poisoning, model theft
\item Red-teaming AI systems and LLMs
\item Model cards, documentation and audit evidence
\item Regulation and standards: EU AI Act, ISO/IEC 42001, NIST AI RMF, DPDP Act
\end{itemize}\end{multicols}

\subsection*{Text \& Reference Books}{\footnotesize\begin{enumerate}[label={[\arabic*]}]
\item S. Barocas, M. Hardt and A. Narayanan, Fairness and Machine Learning: Limitations and Opportunities, MIT Press, 2023.
\item C. Molnar, Interpretable Machine Learning, 2nd ed., 2022 (christophm.github.io/interpretable-ml-book).
\item NIST, Artificial Intelligence Risk Management Framework (AI RMF 1.0), NIST AI 100-1, 2023.
\item European Union, Regulation (EU) 2024/1689 (Artificial Intelligence Act), Oﬀicial Journal of the EU, 2024.
\item ISO/IEC 42001:2023, Information Technology – Artificial Intelligence – Management System.
\item OWASP, Top 10 for Large Language Model Applications, 2025 edition. Emerging Technologies Track Courses 11–15: frontier technologies that combine with AI to create the next generation of enterprise products No. Course Code Why it is emerging 11 Quantum Computing \& Quan- EAI-111 Quantum hardware and hybrid quantum–classical al- tum AI gorithms are reaching cloud access; post-quantum cryptography is now standardised 12 Edge AI, TinyML \& AIoT EAI-112 AI is moving onto devices, sensors and NPUs for la- tency, privacy and cost 13 Physical AI \& Intelligent Robotics EAI-113 Foundation models now drive robots, drones and au- tonomous machines 14 AI-Driven Cybersecurity EAI-114 AI both defends and attacks systems; security opera- tions are being automated 15 Blockchain \& Decentralised AI EAI-115 Verifiable data, model provenance and tokenised trust for AI ecosystems Track Design Principles ▶ Each emerging course is built on the core AI track and shows how the new technology combines with AI in a real product. ▶ Labs run on simulators and low-cost hardware first (quantum simulators, microcontrollers, ROS 2 simulation, isolated security labs, local blockchains), then on cloud or real devices. ▶ Every capstone is scoped as a proof-of-concept an enterprise could pilot, with a business case, risk register and cost estimate. ▶ Content is aligned with national priorities such as the National Quantum Mission, IndiaAI Mission, Digital India and CERT-In guidance.
\end{enumerate}}

\phantomsection\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Course 11\quad Quantum Computing \& Quantum AI}
\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=navy,colframe=navy,arc=2pt,left=8pt,top=5pt,bottom=5pt]{\small\bfseries\color{goldl}COURSE 11 \;|\; EAI-111 \;|\; EMERGING TECHNOLOGIES TRACK}\\[1pt]{\LARGE\bfseries\color{white}Quantum Computing \& Quantum AI}\\[2pt]{\small\itshape\color{goldl}Quantum algorithms, quantum machine learning and quantum-safe enterprise systems}\end{tcolorbox}
\par{\small\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}Y>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}p{2.6cm}@{}}Course Code & EAI-111 & L--T--P--C & 3--0--2--4\\Prerequisites & Linear algebra, probability, EAI-102 Machine Learning & Contact Hours & 45 L + 30 P\\\end{tabularx}}
\subsection*{Core Competencies}\begin{itemize}\small
\item Explain qubits, superposition, entanglement, measurement and quantum circuits mathematically.
\item Implement core quantum algorithms and run them on simulators and cloud quantum hardware.
\item Build hybrid quantum–classical models (variational circuits, quantum kernels) for optimisation and ML.
\item Assess quantum readiness and plan migration to post-quantum cryptography in enterprise systems.
\end{itemize}
\subsection*{Five-Unit Curriculum}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT I}\quad Quantum Computing Foundations\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Classical vs quantum computation; qubits and the Bloch sphere; Dirac notation; superposition, entanglement and measurement; single- and multi-qubit gates; quantum circuits; no-cloning theorem; density matrices; physical qubit technologies (superconducting, trapped-ion, photonic, neutral-atom); India’s National Quantum Mission.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Builds Bell-state and teleportation circuits and verifies measurement statistics on a simulator and real QPU.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT II}\quad Quantum Algorithms\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Deutsch–Jozsa and Bernstein–Vazirani; quantum Fourier transform; phase estimation; Grover’s search and amplitude amplification; Shor’s factoring algorithm and its cryptographic impact; HHL for linear systems; complexity classes (BQP); oracles and circuit compilation.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Implements Grover search and the quantum Fourier transform with circuit-depth analysis.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT III}\quad NISQ Computing and Variational Algorithms\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Noisy intermediate-scale quantum devices; noise models, decoherence and error mitigation; introduction to quantum error correction (surface codes, logical qubits); variational quantum eigensolver (VQE); QAOA for combinatorial optimisation; parameterised circuits and barren plateaus; transpilation and hardware-aware design.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Runs VQE for a small molecule and QAOA for a routing/Max-Cut problem with error mitigation.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT IV}\quad Quantum Machine Learning\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Data encoding (basis, angle, amplitude); quantum kernels and quantum support vector machines; variational quantum classifiers; quantum neural networks; hybrid quantum–classical training with automatic differentiation; quantum generative models; quantum-inspired classical algorithms; honest assessment of quantum advantage claims.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Benchmarks a quantum-kernel SVM and a hybrid quantum neural network against classical models.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT V}\quad Quantum Enterprise Applications and Quantum Security\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Use-cases in portfolio optimisation, logistics, drug and materials discovery; quantum cloud services and cost models; quantum key distribution; post-quantum cryptography (NIST FIPS 203/204/205: ML-KEM, ML-DSA, SLH-DSA); crypto-agility and quantum-readiness roadmaps; building a quantum proof-of-concept business case.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Demonstrates a post-quantum TLS handshake and delivers a quantum-readiness roadmap for an enterprise.\end{tcolorbox}

\phantomsection\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Course 12\quad Edge AI, TinyML \& AIoT}
\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=navy,colframe=navy,arc=2pt,left=8pt,top=5pt,bottom=5pt]{\small\bfseries\color{goldl}COURSE 12 \;|\; EAI-112 \;|\; EMERGING TECHNOLOGIES TRACK}\\[1pt]{\LARGE\bfseries\color{white}Edge AI, TinyML \& AIoT}\\[2pt]{\small\itshape\color{goldl}On-device intelligence for sensors, wearables, vehicles, factories and smart infrastructure}\end{tcolorbox}
\par{\small\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}Y>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}p{2.6cm}@{}}Course Code & EAI-112 & L--T--P--C & 3--0--2--4\\Prerequisites & EAI-103 Deep Learning, embedded C/Python basics & Contact Hours & 45 L + 30 P\\\end{tabularx}}
\subsection*{Core Competencies}\begin{itemize}\small
\item Design AIoT architectures that split work across device, edge gateway and cloud.
\item Compress and deploy neural networks on microcontrollers, NPUs and edge GPUs.
\item Build sensor-data pipelines with real-time inference under power, memory and latency budgets.
\item Operate device fleets securely with over-the-air model updates and on-device privacy.
\end{itemize}
\subsection*{Five-Unit Curriculum}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT I}\quad AIoT Architecture and Edge Computing\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small IoT reference architectures; device–edge–cloud continuum; edge computing vs fog vs cloud; communication protocols (MQTT, CoAP, BLE, LoRaW AN, 5G); latency, bandwidth, power and privacy drivers for edge AI; edge hardware landscape (MCUs, NPUs, Jetson, Coral, smartphone NPUs); smart-city and Industry 4.0 use-cases.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Designs a device-edge-cloud AIoT architecture and streams sensor data over MQTT to a dashboard.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT II}\quad Sensor Data and Embedded ML\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Sensor types (IMU, audio, vision, environmental, vibration); signal processing and feature extraction (FFT, MFCC, spectrograms); data acquisition and labelling on devices; keyword spotting, gesture and anomaly detection; embedded programming basics (Arduino, ESP32, Raspberry Pi); real-time operating systems.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Deploys keyword spotting and vibration anomaly detection on a microcontroller.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT III}\quad Model Optimisation for Constrained Devices\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Post-training and quantisation-aware training (INT8, INT4); pruning and sparsity; knowledge distillation; eﬀicient architectures (MobileNet, EﬀicientNet-Lite, MCUNet); neural architecture search for hardware; memory planning and operator support; benchmarking with MLPerf Tiny.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Produces an INT8 quantisation and pruning study that meets a stated memory, power and latency budget.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT IV}\quad On-Device Generative AI and Federated Learning\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Small language models on phones and laptops; on-device LLM runtimes; vision–language models at the edge; federated learning and on-device personalisation; split computing; privacy-preserving analytics; energy-aware inference scheduling.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Runs a small language model on a Raspberry Pi and federated training across simulated devices.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT V}\quad Edge MLOps, Security and Fleet Management\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Edge MLOps: packaging, OTA updates, A/B rollout and rollback; fleet monitoring and drift on devices; secure boot, trusted execution environments and model encryption; device identity and zero-trust IoT; digital twins of device fleets; case studies in predictive maintenance, agriculture and healthcare wearables.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Delivers OTA model updates with A/B rollout, rollback and secure boot across a device fleet.\end{tcolorbox}

\phantomsection\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Course 13\quad Physical AI \& Intelligent Robotics}
\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=navy,colframe=navy,arc=2pt,left=8pt,top=5pt,bottom=5pt]{\small\bfseries\color{goldl}COURSE 13 \;|\; EAI-113 \;|\; EMERGING TECHNOLOGIES TRACK}\\[1pt]{\LARGE\bfseries\color{white}Physical AI \& Intelligent Robotics}\\[2pt]{\small\itshape\color{goldl}Embodied AI that perceives, plans and acts in the physical world}\end{tcolorbox}
\par{\small\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}Y>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}p{2.6cm}@{}}Course Code & EAI-113 & L--T--P--C & 3--0--2--4\\Prerequisites & EAI-103, EAI-104 recommended, linear algebra & Contact Hours & 45 L + 30 P\\\end{tabularx}}
\subsection*{Core Competencies}\begin{itemize}\small
\item Model robot kinematics, dynamics and control for manipulators and mobile robots.
\item Build perception, localisation and mapping pipelines with ROS 2.
\item Train robot policies in simulation and transfer them to real hardware.
\item Apply robot foundation models and vision–language–action models with safety controls.
\end{itemize}
\subsection*{Five-Unit Curriculum}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT I}\quad Robotics Foundations\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Robot types (manipulators, mobile robots, drones, humanoids, AMRs); coordinate frames and transformations; forward and inverse kinematics; Jacobians; dynamics basics; actuators and sensors (LiDAR, cameras, IMU, encoders, force-torque); PID and model-predictive control; the Physical AI landscape.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Solves forward/inverse kinematics of a 6-DoF arm and tunes PID/MPC control of a mobile robot.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT II}\quad ROS 2 and Robot Software Engineering\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small ROS 2 architecture: nodes, topics, services, actions; DDS middleware; launch files and parameters; URDF robot models; tf2 transforms; visualisation with RViz; real-time and safety-critical software practices; containerised robot software and CI.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Builds ROS 2 nodes, a URDF robot model and a containerised robot software stack with CI.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT III}\quad Perception, Localisation and Navigation\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Sensor fusion and Kalman filters; SLAM (visual and LiDAR); occupancy maps; path planning (A*, RRT, RRT*); the Nav2 navigation stack; obstacle avoidance; 3D perception for grasping; manipulation planning with MoveIt 2.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Achieves autonomous navigation with SLAM + Nav2 and pick-and-place with MoveIt 2.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT IV}\quad Robot Learning and Simulation\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Physics simulation and digital twins of robots; domain randomisation and sim-to-real transfer; RL for locomotion and manipulation; imitation learning and behaviour cloning from demonstrations; diffusion policies; synthetic data generation for robot perception.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Trains a locomotion policy in Isaac Lab and an imitation-learning policy with LeRobot, then tests sim-to-real.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT V}\quad Robot Foundation Models and Deployment\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Vision–language–action (VLA) models; LLMs as robot task planners; natural-language robot interfaces; multi-robot fleet orchestration; functional safety (ISO 10218, ISO 13482, ISO/TS 15066 for cobots); human–robot interaction; case studies in warehouse automation, agriculture, healthcare and inspection drones.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Delivers natural-language task execution via an LLM/VLA planner with safety zones and emergency stop.\end{tcolorbox}

\phantomsection\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Course 14\quad AI-Driven Cybersecurity}
\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=navy,colframe=navy,arc=2pt,left=8pt,top=5pt,bottom=5pt]{\small\bfseries\color{goldl}COURSE 14 \;|\; EAI-114 \;|\; EMERGING TECHNOLOGIES TRACK}\\[1pt]{\LARGE\bfseries\color{white}AI-Driven Cybersecurity}\\[2pt]{\small\itshape\color{goldl}Machine learning and agents for threat detection, response and secure enterprises}\end{tcolorbox}
\par{\small\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}Y>{\bfseries\cellcolor{rowbg}}p{2.3cm}p{2.6cm}@{}}Course Code & EAI-114 & L--T--P--C & 3--0--2--4\\Prerequisites & EAI-102 Machine Learning, computer networks & Contact Hours & 45 L + 30 P\\\end{tabularx}}
\subsection*{Core Competencies}\begin{itemize}\small
\item Map the threat landscape and security operations using MITRE ATT\&CK and kill-chain models.
\item Build ML models for intrusion, malware, phishing, fraud and insider-threat detection.
\item Automate triage and response with LLM-powered security copilots and agents.
\item Harden AI-driven defences against adversarial evasion and meet security compliance needs.
\end{itemize}
\subsection*{Five-Unit Curriculum}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT I}\quad Cybersecurity Foundations for AI Engineers\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small CIA triad; threat actors and attack surfaces; cyber kill chain and MITRE ATT\&CK; network and endpoint security; security operations centre (SOC) workflow; logs, SIEM and telemetry sources; Indian cyber context (CERT-In directions, IT Act); where AI helps defenders and attackers.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Sets up a SOC lab (Wazuh/Elastic) ingesting logs and maps sample attacks to MITRE ATT\&CK.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT II}\quad ML for Threat Detection\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Security datasets and feature engineering from logs, flows and packets; network intrusion detection; malware classification (static and dynamic features); phishing and malicious-URL detection; user and entity behaviour analytics (UEBA); anomaly detection under extreme class imbalance; false-positive cost management.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Builds intrusion, malware and phishing detectors with false-positive cost analysis.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT III}\quad Deep Learning and Graph Methods for Security\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Sequence models for log anomaly detection; Transformers on security logs; graph neural networks for lateralmovement and fraud detection; threat-intelligence knowledge graphs; deep learning for encrypted-traﬀic classification; explainable detections for analysts.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Delivers a Transformer log-anomaly detector and a GNN lateral-movement detector with analyst explanations.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT IV}\quad LLMs and Agents for Security Operations\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small LLM security copilots for alert triage and incident summaries; threat-intelligence extraction from reports; natural-language querying of SIEM data; AI agents for SOAR playbooks with human approval; AI-assisted code review and vulnerability discovery; risks of AI-generated attacks (deepfake phishing, malware generation) and countermeasures.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Builds an LLM triage copilot over SIEM alerts and an agent-driven SOAR playbook with human approval.\end{tcolorbox}
\begin{tcolorbox}[unitbox,title={{\color{gold}UNIT V}\quad Adversarial Robustness, Compliance and Secure Deployment\hfill{\color{gold}9 Hours}}]\small Evasion and poisoning attacks on security models; robust detection design; zero-trust architecture; cloud and container security monitoring; security metrics (MTTD, MTTR); compliance (ISO/IEC 27001, NIST CSF 2.0, India DPDP Act); deploying detection models in production SOCs with feedback loops.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent{\color{unitline}\leaders\hbox to 4pt{\hss\rule[0.6ex]{2pt}{0.4pt}\hss}\hfill\kern0pt}\par\textbf{Hands-on Outcome:} Runs adversarial evasion tests on a detector, hardens it and reports MTTD/MTTR improvements.\end{tcolorbox}

\item C. Chio and D. Freeman, Machine Learning and Security, O’Reilly, 2018.
\item MITRE, ATT\&CK: Adversarial Tactics, Techniques and Common Knowledge, attack.mitre.org.
\item NIST, The NIST Cybersecurity Framework (CSF) 2.0, NIST CSWP 29, 2024.
\item I. Sharafaldin, A. H. Lashkari and A. A. Ghorbani, “Toward Generating a New Intrusion Detection Dataset and Intrusion Traﬀic Characterization,” ICISSP, 2018.
\end{enumerate}}
